      : ""
  }Also judge the run as a whole:
- reciting: did she say the stimulus back close to word-for-word rather than
  retelling it? Full coverage reached by reciting is a failure.
- listing: did she deliver it as a flat run of facts rather than as a connected
  argument?
- coloured: did she react to the stimulus at all — a thought, doubt, example or
  joke — beyond retelling it?
- colour_specific: was that reaction specific to THIS stimulus rather than
  something that would fit any stimulus?
- big_misses: at most three short phrases naming the most important things she
  dropped, in plain words she can act on. Empty if she dropped nothing that
  matters.
- next_time: one or two sentences, addressed to her, saying the single most
  useful thing to do differently on a stimulus like this next time -- what to
  say out loud on purpose, which kind of word she keeps losing, or what her
  Read should have named. Concrete, about THIS run, no praise and no padding.
  Leave it EMPTY when there is nothing to fix: every Detail carried, the Read
  found, the run coloured and in her own words.

Rather than ask first, I pulled the real text. Both rows read back clean from `activity_section_grades`; the file above has all 44 lines.

## One real defect

**"Every cylinder" never fires.** 44 of 44 stimuli carry a `next_time` — including five scored 100 at-the-standard. The prompt does say to leave it empty ([rrcGrading.ts:420](supabase/functions/_shared/rrcGrading.ts:420): "Leave it EMPTY when there is nothing to fix"), but the judge has never once done it. So the branch you asked for — the card saying she hit every cylinder — is dead code in production. It only renders on the lab fixture.

Celia's #10 is the clearest case: 100 percent, and the advice is "State the takeaway out loud" for a takeaway she already stated.

## Voice problems in the judge's wording

- **11 em-dashes** across 44 lines — "out loud—ask yourself", "qualifiers like 'exclusively'—changing it". We just stripped an em-dash from the recall line by hand; the judge is putting them back.
- **"Push past just retelling/restating" appears 10 times**, 8 of them in Celia's 22. It reads as a template, not as reading her run.
- Charles opens 8 lines with "Notice…". Same problem, different verb.

The content is mostly right — Celia #7 catching "health-related benefits" sliding to "enjoyment", Charles #16 on flipping "no new oil fields" to positive — but the phrasing is not your voice and a student who reads all 22 will see the seams.

## The three calls that are yours

1. **The empty-`next_time` fix.** Cheapest version is a hard gate in code, not prompt wording: suppress `nextTime` when the stimulus is at-the-standard, every detail carried, coloured, and the Read matched. Deterministic, no judge trust required, no re-grade needed. Want that?
2. **A voice pass on the prompt** — ban the em-dash, ban the stock openers, and make it name the specific word or move rather than the generic instruction. That does need a deploy and a re-grade to see the effect.
3. **Celia's and Charles's drafts** in [CELIA-RRC-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md](../../wiki/CELIA-RRC-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md) still quote pre-build-f numbers. Celia is now 87 coverage / 17 of 22 at standard; Charles 66 / 6 at standard with 4 lost-it. Refresh them, or leave them parked?

Not done: nothing deployed, nothing pushed, no temp files deleted. Two new temp scripts in `scripts/` (`_nexttime_tmp.ts`, `_nexttime_md_tmp.ts`) join the deletion list awaiting your confirmation.

import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

import { nextStep } from "../recall/nextStep";
import { listModelRuns, signSectionRecording } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { raiseDispute } from "../recall/disputes";
import type { ModelRun } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade, SectionStimulusGrade } from "../api/types";

/**
 * What a finished Read, Recall, Colour section produces.
 *
 * Every stimulus graded on its own merits, showing what she missed and what she
 * got perfect. Three rules from the decisions behind it shape what is on
 * screen:
 *
 * - **A band, not a percentage.** A correct run by the author of the standard
 *   sits at a median of 93% and clears 82% nine times in ten on the live
 *   judge (ADR 0035; ADR 0018 measured 95 and 86 by hand), so a raw score
 *   against an implied 100 tells a student she failed at something she did
 *   properly (ADR 0018). This card's own docstring said that and then printed
 *   the percentage as the hero in 48px type with no band at all. The band leads
 *   now; the number is under it, because "what did I actually get" is a fair
 *   question that just does not go first.
 * - **The band answers "was this good"; the missed Details answer "what did I
 *   drop".** Both are shown even at the standard, which is what stops the top
 *   band reading as nothing left to learn.
 * - **Everything measured is either shown or deliberately not.** The grader
 *   computed her transcript, her dead air, whether each reaction was specific,
 *   whether the judge credited words she never said, and how many stimuli it
 *   could not mark, and rendered none of it. A number a student cannot
 *   interrogate is a number she has to take on faith.
 *
 * Internal identifiers never appear. She sees "Stimulus 4", never the capture's
 * own slug, and never the decoy or grader-version machinery.
 */

interface SectionReportCardProps {
  grade: SectionGrade;
  onDone?: () => void;
  /** Injected in tests and in the lab, which has no database behind it. */
  loadModelRuns?: (sourceSection: string) => Promise<Record<string, ModelRun>>;
}

const BAND: Record<SectionStimulusGrade["band"], { label: string; cls: string; dot: string }> = {
  "at-the-standard": {
    label: "At the standard",
    cls: "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
    dot: "bg-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  },
  "nearly-there": { label: "Nearly there", cls: "text-[hsl(38_92%_34%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(38_92%_44%)]" },
  thin: { label: "Thin", cls: "text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(24_85%_50%)]" },
  "lost-it": { label: "Worth another read", cls: "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]", dot: "bg-[hsl(350_80%_55%)]" },
};

/**
 * What a recited stimulus wears instead of a band.
 *
 * ADR 0018 says coverage reached by reciting is a failure, and the row said the
 * opposite. In the audit's run, stimulus 3 was read out verbatim during The
 * Recall, was correctly flagged, and still sat on the card as "At the standard,
 * 20 of 20" in green. The recital sentence was inside the expanded row, which
 * is the half she has to go looking for, and the band is the half she reads.
 *
 * Not a fifth band, because it is not a worse score -- a recital can carry every
 * detail, and that is exactly the problem. It is the statement that the number
 * beside it did not come from memory, so it is neutral rather than red: the
 * point is the method, not a mark against her.
 */
const RECITED = {
  label: "Said back word for word",
  cls: "text-muted-foreground",
  dot: "bg-muted-foreground",
};

function minutes(seconds: number): string {
  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  const s = Math.round(seconds % 60);
  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

}

[external_agent_tool_result]
 *
 * Rendered from the walkthrough's own shape rather than through a markdown
 * library: the document is exactly two kinds of line, a `> ` quote holding the
 * chunk as it was read aloud and a paragraph holding the recall of it, and
 * parsing those two directly means no dependency and nothing that can render
 * markup the vault put in a file. Anything unexpected falls through as a
 * paragraph, which is the safe direction.
 *
 * Shown open, under her own words, so the comparison is the default rather than
 * a click. "You dropped the word only" is a correction; her sentence sitting
 * above his is the lesson.
 */
/**
 * The walkthrough cut into (read, recall) pairs, one per authored line.
 *
 * The document is a `> ` quote holding the line as read, then one or more
 * paragraphs recalling it. Paired up, it can sit under HER line rather than
 * at the bottom of the row, which is the comparison the drill is about: what
 * she said for line 2, and what the standard says for line 2, together.
 */
function walkthroughPairs(md: string): { read: string; recall: string }[] {
  const pairs: { read: string; recall: string }[] = [];
  for (const raw of md.split("\n")) {
    const line = raw.trim();
    if (!line) continue;
    if (line.startsWith("> ")) pairs.push({ read: line.slice(2), recall: "" });
    else if (pairs.length) pairs[pairs.length - 1].recall += (pairs[pairs.length - 1].recall ? " " : "") + line;
    else pairs.push({ read: "", recall: line });
  }
  return pairs;
}

/**
 * Whether a stimulus hit every cylinder: every Detail, the Read, a reaction
 * of her own, in her own words and not as a list. The one case where the
 * card has nothing to ask of her, and says so rather than going quiet.
 */
function everyCylinder(s: SectionStimulusGrade): boolean {
  return (
    !s.ungraded &&
    s.missed.length === 0 &&
    s.readMatches === true &&
    s.coloured &&
    s.colourSpecific &&
    !s.reciting &&
    !s.listing
  );
}

function Walkthrough({ md }: { md: string }) {
  const lines = md.split("\n").filter((l) => l.trim());
  return (
    <div className="mt-5 rounded-xl border border-border bg-background/40 p-4">
      <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-wide text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]">
        Done right
      </p>
      <div className="mt-3 space-y-3">
        {lines.map((line, i) =>
          line.startsWith("> ") ? (
            <p
              key={i}
              className="border-l-2 border-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))] pl-3 text-sm italic leading-relaxed text-foreground"
            >
              {line.slice(2)}
            </p>
          ) : (
            <p key={i} className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-muted-foreground">
              {line}
            </p>
          ),
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

## The apology / accountability move

One of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainl...

{

  "product": "# Product\r\n\r\n<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->\r\n\r\n## Scope\r\n\r\nApp-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.\r\n\r\nA rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.\r\n\r\nThe repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.\r\n\r\n## Platform\r\n\r\nweb\r\n\r\n## Users\r\n\r\n**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.\r\n\r\n**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.\r\n\r\n**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.\r\n\r\n## Product Purpose\r\n\r\nLSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.\r\n\r\nFor a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.\r\n\r\nFor Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.\r\n\r\nSuccess is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.\r\n\r\n## Positioning\r\n\r\n**A real coach is in it.**\r\n\r\nThe claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.\r\n\r\n## Operating Context\r\n\r\nThe platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.\r\n\r\n- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.\r\n- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).\r\n- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.\r\n- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.\r\n- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.\r\n\r\n## Capabilities and Constraints\r\n\r\nConfirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, ...

> **Additional context needed**: what the interface is trying to accomplish.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser automation tools are available AND the target is a viewabl...

# Activity Centre — surface brief

## Scope and mode

The Activity Centre wing: the home list (`/activities`), each Activity's intro, and the eight worlds that fill the runner's left column. Mode: **Experience** for the worlds (the student is inside the room the skill lives in), **Operate** for the answer loop on the right, which stays on the module's cream surface untouched.

## Direction (locked 2026-08-21)

**Eight worlds, eight universes.** Every Activity opens into a fully committed physical scene, saturated and material, scoped behind its wrapper class so nothing leaks into the shell. This is a deliberate, owner-approved departure from the app-wide monochrome-editorial restraint: the Centre is the one wing where the room IS the teaching instrument. The Coach and answer loop remain the constant; the room changes.

| World | Activity | Scene | Display face | Key hues |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | obsidian void split by an aurora; survey-grid floor; prismatic glass plate | Italiana | ultraviolet 275°, polar cyan 190° |
| Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | Victorian naturalist's study; green wall, parchment bench, bell jar on brass plinth | EB Garamond | botanical green 162°, brass 40°, madder 8° |
| Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | celestial cartographer's dome; nebulae, twinkling stars, gilt ribs | Marcellus | indigo 233°, gold 45°, comet cyan 187°, rose 330° |
| Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | midnight rail yard; sodium floods, fog, converging track fan, solari board | Barlow Condensed | steel-night 213°, sodium 36°, signal emerald 158° |
| Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | stained-glass workshop; lamp cone, dust, tinted panes in lead cames | Young Serif | umber 24°, lamp gold 40°, glass tints rotating |
| Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | torchlit porphyry rotunda; bronze protractor inlaid in the floor | Cinzel | porphyry 356°, torchlight 36°, bronze 30° |
| The Press | Dumb it Down | hot-metal print shop; ink rollers, iron frame, vermilion proof red | Bevan | iron 20°, paper cream 42°, vermilion 14° |
| The Loom | Spot the Callback | indigo dye-house; warp threads, linen sheets with selvage, saffron thread | Alegreya | indigo 236°, linen 43°, saffron 42° |

Statement/body text across dark worlds runs Spectral 300; micro-labels stay on the shared `--ac-mono`.

## Standing rules

- **Scene layers are furniture, not content.** Every world's atmosphere lives in one `aria-hidden` layer stack (`.po-room`, `.sl-room`, …) painted below `z-index: 2` content; tested DOM never gains or loses nodes for scenery.
- **Meaning colors survive grayscale.** Hue is atmosphere on top of geometry/weight/position, never the only carrier of a verdict. Emerald still means "earned/true" wherever it appears.
- **One orchestrated entrance per world**, durations pinned by tests (1800–2500ms), always skippable, `useReducedMotion` short-circuits to `onDone()`.
- **Reduced motion**: every world CSS ends with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block; continuous loops stop rather than speed up.
- **The home is a hall of doorways.** Each `ActivityCard` carries a pure-CSS diorama of its world (`activity-scenes.css`, keyed by slug); the card's facts stay on the module paper below the scene. The intro borrows `--act-accent` from its world via the registry.
- **Registry accents** (`worlds/registry.tsx`) are each world's signature hue and feed the card eyebrow + intro accent.

## Unresolved

- Negation Station is not in `SEED_ACTIVITIES`, so its diorama only shows once assigned.
- Progress ornaments (chips, plaques, mileposts, seals…) remain unreachable from the runner; feeding them real verdicts is its own piece of work.

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
/**
 * Cutting one stimulus's speech into the LINES she was rephrasing.
 *
 * The drill is line by line: she reads one line silently, looks away, says that
 * line back in her own words, then returns to the text for the next one. The
 * app never asks her which line she is on (ADR 0013) and it never will -- a tap
 * per line is ninety taps a section and one mistimed tap desyncs the whole
 * recording. So the split is worked out AFTERWARDS, from two things the run
 * already leaves behind:
 *
 * - **The silence.** She is silent while reading, so every spoken line arrives
 *   as its own burst with a gap in front of it. Bursts are the candidate units,
 *   and nothing here assumes she talks continuously.
 * - **The order.** She works top to bottom, so bursts map to lines in order.
 *   That makes this an alignment, not a classification: a burst can only belong
 *   to the line it is nearest to in sequence, which is far more robust than
 *   asking which line a paraphrase "sounds like".
 *
 * Wording is the tiebreak, not the decision. A rephrase shares content words
 * with the line it came from, so overlap picks between the two lines an
 * ordering leaves ambiguous.
 *
 * Nothing in this file is a boundary she is GRADED against. Coverage stays per
 * stimulus (ADR 0013); this exists so she can be shown, and hear, which line
 * each part of her run belongs to.
 */

export interface TimedWord {
  word: string;
  /** Seconds from the start of the recording, as the transcriber reports them. */
  start: number;
  end: number;
}

export interface Utterance {
  text: string;
  startMs: number;
  endMs: number;
}

export interface LineSpan {
  /** 1-based line number within the stimulus, matching the authored chunk. */
  n: number;
  /** The line as written. */
  text: string;
  /** What she said for it, joined in order. Empty when she said nothing. */
  spoken: string;
  startMs?: number;
  endMs?: number;
}

/**
 * The gap that separates one spoken line from the next, in milliseconds.
 *
 * She has to look back at the screen and read the next line silently before she
 * speaks again, so the real gaps are seconds long. This is set below the
 * shortest of those and above the pauses inside a sentence; it is a default
 * rather than a constant because it is the one number here that wants tuning
 * against real recordings.
 */
=== transcribe ===
28: * - The transcript comes from Deepgram Nova-3, the same engine that read the
54:const ASR_ENGINE = "deepgram/nova-3";
91: * Transcribe the recording Deepgram can fetch at `audioUrl`.
98: * by re-driving, because every re-drive dies the same way. Deepgram fetches
106:    "https://api.deepgram.com/v1/listen?model=nova-3&punctuate=true&smart_format=true",
114:  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Deepgram ${res.status}: ${JSON.stringify(body).slice(0, 300)}`);
365:        transcriber: Deno.env.get("DEEPGRAM_API_KEY") ? "deepgram" : "missing DEEPGRAM_API_KEY",

.qf-trigger-label {
  flex: 1;
  min-width: 0;
  overflow: hidden;
  text-overflow: ellipsis;
  white-space: nowrap;
}


/* Phones: the masthead is already carrying the greeting and the due pill, so
   the label and the shortcut hint fall away and the trigger becomes an icon.
   The button itself is unchanged — same control, same accessible name — which
   is why there is no second trigger to hide. */
@media (max-width: 720px) {
  .qf-trigger {
    min-width: 0;
    padding: 10px;
  }

  .qf-trigger-label,
}

/* ── portal, scrim, panel ─────────────────────────────────────────────── */

.qf-portal {
  /* flex-start, not the default stretch: a stretched panel paints its surface
     down to the bottom of the viewport and the content sits in the top third of
     an empty card. The panel must be exactly as tall as what is in it. */
  align-items: flex-start;
  bottom: 0;
  display: flex;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

/**
 * A preview harness for the section launch surface. Development only.
 *
 * It exists so the component can be looked at in a real browser, in every state
 * and at every breakpoint, without signing in and without a graded section
 * sitting in the database. Only the network is stubbed: `window.fetch` is
 * replaced before anything imports the Supabase client, so the component, the
 * client helper and the derivation all run exactly as they do in the product.
 *
 * Not routed and not imported by the app. Reached at
 * `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` on the dev server. It is not an entry
 * point in the Vite build, which only builds `index.html`, so it never ships.
 *
 * States: ?state=runs|oneRun|empty|failed|noHistory|library  ·  ?theme=light|dark
 */
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";
// The section JSONs deliberately ship an empty instructions block -- the rules
// are authored once, in one file, and copied in at seed time -- so the harness
// reads the same instructions file the migration seeds.
import sectionInstructions from "../features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../features/activity-centre/api/types";

const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
const state = params.get("state") ?? "runs";
const theme = params.get("theme") ?? "light";

function stim(item_order: number, over: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  return {
    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    item_order,
    carried: 3,
    total: 3,
    percent: 100,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0,
    spoken: "she said something here",
    ...over,
  };
}

/**
 * The Report Card, against the trial run's own numbers.
 *
 * The card is only reachable behind a signed-in student who has finished a
 * twenty-five minute recorded section and waited for the grader, which made it
 * the least-looked-at screen in the product and is how it shipped printing a
 * raw percentage as its hero directly under a docstring forbidding exactly
 * that. The fixture below is the PT102.S3 trial run: 91% mean Coverage, 17 of
 * 22 at the standard, one deliberate recite, one stimulus with seven seconds of
 * dead air, four uncoloured.
 *
 * The Clip button appears and does nothing here on purpose. There is no
 * recording behind a fixture, and a lab that faked one would be the one place
 * a broken player could hide.
 */
function ReportPanel() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState<"clean" | "faults" | "partial">("faults");

  const grade = useMemo(() => makeLabGrade(attempt), [attempt]);

  return (
    <div>
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 border-b border-border px-6 py-3">
        <Tabs
          options={["clean", "faults", "partial"] as const}
          value={attempt}
          onChange={(v) => setAttempt(v)}
          label="Run"
        />
        <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
          clean: everything at the standard · faults: the trial run · partial: stimuli we could not
          mark
        </p>
      </div>
      <div className="px-4 py-10">
        <SectionReportCard
          grade={grade}
          /*
           * The lab has no database, so the model runs are named rather than
           * looked up. The player button appears and does nothing, which is the
           * point: a lab that faked a working player would be the one place a
           * broken one could hide.
           *
           * The walkthrough is different, and is real text rather than a stub.
           * It is the part a student actually reads, it is the part most likely
           * to look wrong at some width or some length, and a lab that showed
           * the buttons but not the document would be checking the half that
           * matters least.
           */
          loadModelRuns={async () =>
            Object.fromEntries(
              grade.stimuli.map((st) => [
                st.slug,
                {
                  audio: { bucket: "model-runs", path: `${st.slug}.mp3` },
                  walkthrough:
                    "> Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so " +
                    "that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they " +
                    "do well.\n\n" +
                    "So a management consultant is telling us how to run the company, and " +
                    "the idea is to move people around so they are doing the things they " +
[/external_agent_tool_result]

/*
 * The row that tells her what to change, and the standard beside her words.
 *
 * Every number on the card was a description. These are the three things a
 * student can act on: what to do next time, what the Read should have named,
 * and what the standard says for the very line she was rephrasing.
 */
describe("what to do next time", () => {
  it("shows the judge's advice on a stimulus with something to fix", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{ ...grade, stimuli: [stimulus({ nextTime: "Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some." })] }}
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(screen.getByText("Next time")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText("Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some.")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says every cylinder when there is nothing to fix, and gives no advice", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          stimuli: [
            stimulus({
              percent: 100,
              carried: 12,
              total: 12,
              missed: [],
              readGiven: true,
              readMatches: true,
              nextTime: "",
            }),
          ],
        }}
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(screen.getByText(/Every cylinder on this one/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText("Next time")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("does not call a recital every cylinder, whatever it carried", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          stimuli: [
            stimulus({ percent: 100, missed: [], readGiven: true, readMatches: true, reciting: true, nextTime: "" }),

        }}

Four references pulled. Linear ships a near-black `#08090A` console with indigo accent; Gong deep violet with magenta; Descript a tinted off-white that is pointedly not cream. [external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Retune the palette to a single signal hue
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu && python - <<'EOF'
import re,io
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
s=s.replace(""" * - **Signal (indigo-violet)** is sound. It marks the line currently playing
 *   and nothing else, so the only saturated colour that moves on the page is
 *   the playhead.
 * - **Ember** is attention. It marks what slipped, and nothing else.""",
""" * There is exactly ONE saturated hue on the whole card, and it means sound.
 * Signal red marks the line currently playing and nothing else, so the only
 * colour that moves is the playhead. It is the transport light on a console,
 * which is what this page is: she is listening to a recording.
 *
 * What she dropped takes no hue at all. It is marked by shape, a hollow chip
 * against her held ones, which is legible in grayscale and to a colour-blind
 * reader and keeps the page from turning into a heat map of failure. A page
 * that paints every miss red is a page she reads once.
 *
 * Deliberately NOT indigo-violet: Linear ships #5E6AD2 on #08090A and Gong
 * ships magenta on #1D0254, so a dark product surface with a violet accent is
 * the second reflex, not a decision (references pulled 2026-09-08).""")
s=s.replace("""  --rc-signal: oklch(0.55 0.19 286);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.72 0.16 286);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.945 0.032 286);
  --rc-ember: oklch(0.54 0.15 42);
  --rc-ember-soft: oklch(0.955 0.032 42);""",
"""  --rc-signal: oklch(0.575 0.196 28);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.7 0.17 30);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.955 0.028 28);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.99 0.005 28);""")
s=s.replace("oklch(0.99 0.005 286)","var(--rc-signal-ink)")
# the ledger's lost-it mark loses its hue: shape carries it
s=s.replace("""/* Lost it: a hollow ring in ember, the one place the ledger takes a hue. */
.rc__mark--lost-it {
  border-color: var(--rc-ember);
  border-radius: 50%;
}""",
"""/* Lost it: a hollow ring. Empty, and round against three squares, so it reads
   as a different kind of thing rather than as a worse colour. */
.rc__mark--lost-it {
  border-radius: 50%;
}""")
s=s.replace(""".rc__chip--dropped {
  background: var(--rc-ember-soft);
  color: var(--rc-ember);
}""",
"""/* Hollow against the held ones. Shape, not hue: see the palette note above. */
.rc__chip--dropped {
  background: transparent;
  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--rc-hairline);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
}""")
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print("signal:", "--rc-signal: oklch(0.575 0.196 28)" in s, "| ember gone:", "--rc-ember" not in s)
EOF
[/external_agent_tool_call]

describe("what the student sees", () => {
  it("leads with a band, not a raw verdict", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={grade} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    expect(screen.getAllByText("At the standard").length).toBe(2);
    expect(screen.getByText("Thin")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says how she did against her own time goal", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={grade} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    expect(screen.getByText("23:00 against a 25 minute goal")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("names the stimuli by position, never by the capture's identifier", () => {
  };

  it("does not call it a success on the row she reads", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={recited} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    expect(screen.queryByText("At the standard")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("wears no success colour", () => {
    const { container } = render(<SectionReportCard grade={recited} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    const row = container.querySelector("ol li");
    expect(row?.innerHTML).not.toContain("ac-emerald-strong");
  });

  it("names the recital on the collapsed row, not only inside it", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={recited} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    // Present before anything is expanded.
    expect(screen.getByText(/said back word for word/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("still shows what it carried, because that reading is true", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard grade={recited} loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} />);
    expect(screen.getByText("20 of 20")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("does not congratulate it inside the row either", () => {
describe("the evidence behind the number", () => {
  const withSection = (over: Partial<SectionGrade["section"]>, stimuliOver?: SectionStimulusGrade[]) =>
    ({ ...grade, section: { ...grade.section, ...over }, stimuli: stimuliOver ?? grade.stimuli }) as SectionGrade;

  it("leads with the run's own band, not the percentage", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ mean_coverage: 91 })} />);
    // 91 is at the standard. The card used to print "91%" in 48px type with no
    // band at all, which is the exact thing its own docstring forbade.
    const hero = document.querySelector(".ac-display");
    expect(hero?.textContent).toBe("At the standard");
    expect(hero?.textContent).not.toMatch(/%/);
  });

  it("still says what she actually got, underneath", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ mean_coverage: 91 })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/91% of the details carried/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows The Read beside Coverage", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ read_given_count: 3, read_matched_count: 2, no_read_count: 0 })}
      />,
    );
    expect(screen.getByText("Understood")).toBeInTheDocument();
    // Against every stimulus graded, not against the ones she gave a Read on.
    expect(screen.getByText("2 of 4")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says the goal was missed on its condition, not on the clock", () => {
    // The spec's own rule: the goal counts only with every stimulus recalled,
    // coloured and given a Read. Finishing early with four skipped was being
    // reported as making the goal.
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ within_time: true, within_goal: false })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/Inside the time, but the goal only counts/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says plainly when we could not mark part of the run", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ ungraded_count: 2 })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/2 stimuli could not be marked/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/ours, not yours/)).toBeInTheDocument();

function makeLabGrade(kind: "clean" | "faults" | "partial"): SectionGrade {
  // `over.slug` is the stimulus NUMBER, not the slug. Spread last it would
  // overwrite the slug it was meant to build, and the run history would then
  // look up Details for a stimulus called "7" and find none.
  const stim = ({ slug: n, ...over }: Partial<SectionStimulusGrade>): SectionStimulusGrade => ({
    slug: `PT102_S3_Q${n ?? "1"}`,
    carried: 20,
    total: 21,
    percent: 95,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    readGiven: true,
    readMatches: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0.4,
    spoken:
      "so the director says their consultant wants people moved around so everyone is doing what they like and what they are good at",
    recall_window_ms: { start: 12000, end: 48000 },
    stimulus_text:
      "Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they do well. This, she says, will \"increase productivity by fully exploiting our available resources.\"",

    readReason: "She said the consultant may be assuming everyone wants to move, which is a different move from the one the argument turns on.",

description: Capture full-page screenshots with Playwright
command: SHOT_DIR="C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/763f2bd6-f34e-4336-b164-2d9ce5e6c18f/scratchpad"
cat > scripts/_rc_shots.mjs <<EOF
import { chromium } from "@playwright/test";
const OUT = "$SHOT_DIR";
const base = "http://localhost:8082/src/dev/report-card-preview.html";
const b = await chromium.launch();
async function shot(name, url, width, height, prep) {
  const p = await b.newPage({ viewport: { width, height } });
  await p.goto(url, { waitUntil: "networkidle" });
  await p.waitForTimeout(900);
  if (prep) await prep(p);
  await p.screenshot({ path: \`\${OUT}/\${name}.png\`, fullPage: true });
  const errs = [];
  p.on("pageerror", (e) => errs.push(String(e)));
  console.log(name, "->", await p.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.scrollHeight), "px");
  await p.close();
}
await shot("rc-desktop", base, 1180, 1000);
await shot("rc-playing", base + "?playing=1", 1180, 1000);
await shot("rc-mobile", base, 390, 844);
await shot("rc-clean", base + "?state=clean", 1180, 1000);
await b.close();
EOF
npx vite-node scripts/_rc_shots.mjs 2>&1 | tail -10

cat > scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs <<'EOF'
import { chromium } from "@playwright/test";
const b = await chromium.launch();
const p = await b.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1180, height: 1000 } });
await p.goto("http://localhost:8082/src/dev/report-card-preview.html", { waitUntil: "networkidle" });
await p.waitForTimeout(800);
const out = await p.evaluate(() => {
  const lum = (c) => {
    const [r, g, bl] = c.map((v) => { v /= 255; return v <= 0.03928 ? v / 12.92 : ((v + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4; });
    return 0.2126 * r + 0.7152 * g + 0.0722 * bl;
  };
  const parse = (s) => (s.match(/[\d.]+/g) || []).slice(0, 3).map(Number);
  const bgOf = (el) => {
    let n = el;
    while (n) { const c = getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor; if (c && !/rgba\(0, 0, 0, 0\)|transparent/.test(c)) return c; n = n.parentElement; }
    return "rgb(255,255,255)";
  };
  const ratio = (el) => {
    const f = lum(parse(getComputedStyle(el).color)); const g = lum(parse(bgOf(el)));
    const [hi, lo] = f > g ? [f, g] : [g, f];
    return +(((hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05)).toFixed(2));
  };
  const samples = [];
  const seen = new Set();
  document.querySelectorAll(".rc *").forEach((el) => {
    if (!el.textContent?.trim() || el.children.length) return;
    const cs = getComputedStyle(el);
    const size = parseFloat(cs.fontSize);
    const k = `${cs.color}|${bgOf(el)}|${size}`;
    if (seen.has(k)) return; seen.add(k);
    const large = size >= 24 || (size >= 18.66 && +cs.fontWeight >= 700);
    const need = large ? 3 : 4.5;
    const r = ratio(el);
    samples.push({ text: el.textContent.trim().slice(0, 34), size, ratio: r, need, pass: r >= need });
  });
  return samples.sort((a, b) => a.ratio - b.ratio);
});
console.log(JSON.stringify(out.filter((s) => !s.pass), null, 1));
console.log("checked", out.length, "| failures", out.filter((s) => !s.pass).length);
await b.close();
EOF
npx vite-node scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs 2>&1 | tail -40
[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Fix the colour parsing and re-measure contrast
command: python - <<'PY'
p='scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
s=s.replace('''  const parse = (s) => (s.match(/[\\d.]+/g) || []).slice(0, 3).map(Number);''',
'''  // Chrome serializes computed colours as oklch(...) when the author wrote
  // oklch, so they are converted through a canvas rather than parsed.
  const cv = document.createElement("canvas");
  cv.width = cv.height = 1;
  const ctx = cv.getContext("2d", { willReadFrequently: true });
  const parse = (s) => {
    ctx.clearRect(0, 0, 1, 1);
    ctx.fillStyle = "#000";
    ctx.fillStyle = s;
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, 1, 1);
    const d = ctx.getImageData(0, 0, 1, 1).data;
    return [d[0], d[1], d[2]];
  };''')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('ok')

description: Record the locked Report Card direction in the surface brief
command: cat >> .impeccable/surfaces/src-features-activity-centre.md <<'EOF'

## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)

Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream and
not emerald** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI looking", "I don't
want emerald"), which makes this the second sanctioned departure from the wing's
surface after the RRC door.

- **Two grounds, one card.** A dark lacquered console holds the verdict, the
  three figures and the ledger; cool paper (`oklch(0.976 0.0035 268)`, a faint
  blue cast, never beige) holds everything she reads at length.
- **One saturated hue, and it means sound.** Signal red marks the line currently
  playing and nothing else. Deliberately not indigo-violet: Linear ships
  `#5E6AD2` on `#08090A` and Gong magenta on `#1D0254`, so dark-plus-violet is
  the second reflex rather than a decision (references pulled 2026-09-08).
- **Verdicts are fill, not hue.** The ledger's four marks differ by how full
  they are; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow circle. Everything
  survives grayscale, which is what lets the page carry no other colour.
- **One stimulus at a time.** Twenty-two accordion rows became a ledger of
  twenty-two marks plus one open stimulus in a fixed order: the passage, what
  you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.
- **Contrast is measured, not assumed.** `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs` converts
  computed colours through a canvas (Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`) and
  every text node clears 4.5:1 on its own ground. Both faint inks sit where they
  do because that is where they pass.
- **Type ramp:** Fraunces display, Lexend body, IBM Plex Mono labels. The design
  detector reports the sizes as outside DESIGN.md; that is this sanctioned world,
  not drift, the same exception the RRC door's `--rd-*` tokens carry.
- **Harness:** `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders it at twenty-two
  stimuli without `App.tsx`. `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to
  freeze the playhead on a line.
EOF
echo appended
[/external_agent_tool_call]

firecrawl agent "Research UI patterns for a student report card that plays back an audio recording with a transcript that highlights line by line, and grades 22 items. First: how do Descript, Otter.ai, Sonix, Trint, Happy Scribe and Gong visually highlight the transcript line or word that is currently playing - what granularity, what treatment (background fill, underline, moving bar), and what colour. Second: how do Duolingo, Grammarly, Gong and Khan Academy colour per-item feedback so that a page full of misses does not read as a wall of red - what colour means good, what means missed. p='src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.tsx'

# 1. A natural phrase for how much came back, replacing the Detail arithmetic.
anchor = '''/**
 * What she actually did well on this stimulus, in specifics.'''
helper = '''/**
 * How much of it came back, in words.
 *
 * A Detail is OUR unit. It is how the judge is asked to mark, it is what the
 * coverage percentage is computed from, and a student has never seen one
 * enumerated: she read a passage and said it back. Printing "20 of 21 details"
 * hands her our machinery and asks her to care about the one number on the page
 * she cannot check (Joshua, 2026-09-08). The band says how it went; this says
 * how much of it she kept, the way a person would say it.
 */
function howMuch(percent: number): string {
  if (percent >= 100) return "All of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 90) return "Almost all of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 75) return "Most of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 55) return "About half of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 25) return "Some of it came back.";
  return "Not much of it came back.";
}

/**
 * What she actually did well on this stimulus, in specifics.'''
assert anchor in t; t=t.replace(anchor, helper, 1)

# 2. whatHeld drops the count line entirely and keeps the qualitative specifics.
a = '''  if (!s.reciting) {
    // Nothing about coverage here when she dropped nothing: "What slipped"
    // says exactly that, directly below, and the two sections sat next to each
    // other stating the same fact in two different sentences.
    if (s.missed.length > 0 && s.total > 0 && s.carried / s.total >= 0.8)
      held.push(`${s.carried} of the ${s.total} details came back.`);
    const whole = s.lines?.filter((l) => l.verdict === "strong").length ?? 0;
    if (whole > 0) held.push(`${whole} ${whole === 1 ? "line" : "lines"} came back whole.`);
  }'''
b = '''  if (!s.reciting) {
    // How much came back is said once, on the row above, in words. It is not
    // repeated here as a fraction.
    const whole = s.lines?.filter((l) => l.verdict === "strong").length ?? 0;
    if (whole === 1) held.push("One line came back whole.");
    else if (whole > 1 && whole === s.lines?.length) held.push("Every line came back whole.");
    else if (whole > 1) held.push(`${whole} of the lines came back whole.`);
  }'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)

# 3. The stimulus header says how much came back rather than counting Details.
a = '''              <p className="text-sm tabular-nums text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                {s.carried} of {s.total} details
              </p>'''
b = '''              <p className="text-sm text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">{howMuch(s.percent)}</p>'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)

# 4. The console figures lose the word "details".
a = '''                : "of the details, said back"'''
b = '''                : "of what was on the page, said back in your own words"'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)
a = '''                ? `of the details, across the ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} that saved`'''
b = '''                ? `said back in your own words, across the ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} that saved`'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)
a = '''                  ? `Reads that found the move. You gave none at all on ${section.no_read_count}.`
                  : "Reads that found the move"'''
b = '''                  ? `where you found the move. On ${section.no_read_count} you did not give a Read at all.`
                  : "where you found the move the argument turns on"'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)

# 5. "What slipped" with nothing missed no longer needs to count either.
a = '''                        {s.reciting
                          ? "Every detail is in there, which is what reciting does rather than what you did."
                          : "You carried every detail in this one."}'''
b = '''                        {s.reciting
                          ? "It is all in there, which is what saying it back word for word does rather than what you did."
                          : "Nothing slipped on this one. You kept the whole thing."}'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)

a = '''                        Every cylinder on this one: every detail, the Read, and a reaction of your
                        own, in your own words.'''
b = '''                        Every cylinder on this one. You kept the whole thing, you found the move it
                        turns on, and you had something of your own to say about it, all in your own
                        words.'''

open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(t)
print("component ok")

description: Extract findings from all four jobs
command: P=$(cygpath -w "$(pwd)"); python - "$P" <<'PY'
import json,re,sys,os,glob
base=sys.argv[1]
for f in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(base,'.firecrawl','rc','*.json'))):
    raw=open(f,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read()
    name=os.path.basename(f)
    cands=[]
    for c in re.findall(r'\{(?:[^{}]|\{(?:[^{}]|\{(?:[^{}]|\{[^{}]*\})*\})*\})*\}', raw, re.S):
        try: cands.append(json.loads(c))
        except Exception: pass
    good=[c for c in cands if isinstance(c,dict) and any(k in c or k in c.get('data',{}) for k in ('palettes','rules','apps','principles','options','recommendation','components'))]
    print("="*8,name,len(raw),"json blocks",len(cands),"useful",len(good))
    if not good:
        print("   tail:", re.sub(r'\s+',' ',raw[-260:]))
        continue
    d=max(good,key=lambda x:len(json.dumps(x))); d=d.get('data',d)
    for k,v in d.items():
        print(" ##",k)
        if isinstance(v,list):
            for it in v[:10]: print("   -", json.dumps(it,ensure_ascii=False)[:230])
        else: print("   ",str(v)[:300])
PY

## assistant

Six Firecrawl agent jobs are running server-side. While they finish, here's where things stand.

**Done and verified**

The Report Card is rebuilt. 52/52 of its own tests pass, 2,685 across the Activity Centre, typecheck clean, and every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser.

- **Structure**: 22 collapsible rows became a ledger of 22 marks on a console, plus one stimulus open at a time in a fixed order — the passage, what you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time. Same order every time, so by the third she stops reading the labels.
- **Playback**: the transcript follows the sound. The grader already stored a start and end for each line she spoke, so no word timings and no re-grade were needed — the playhead drives a highlight down the lines as her recording plays. Her voice and the standard's sit in one transport rather than two scattered links.
- **Specific congratulation**: a "What you held" section read off measured facts, ahead of the correction rather than after it.

**Just changed on your note about details**

"20 of 21 details" is gone everywhere. A Detail is our unit — she never saw one enumerated — so it now reads "Almost all of it came back", "Nothing slipped on this one. You kept the whole thing." The console says "of what was on the page, said back in your own words" instead of "of the details".

**One correction to something I told you earlier**

I said the "Every cylinder" branch was dead code. That was wrong. It gates on measured criteria, not on the judge's empty string, so it fires correctly — Celia never triggers it because she gave no Read on any of the 22, which is the right answer. The real finding is narrower: the judge never returns an empty `next_time`, but the card doesn't depend on that.

**Still open**

The colour pass. You're right that it's too grey — I built it near-monochrome on the reasoning that verdicts should survive grayscale, and that's too austere for what you asked for. The most useful thing to come back so far is Grammarly's mechanic: colour by *category* of feedback, not by severity. Blue for clarity, green for engagement, purple for tone, red only for outright errors. That's how a page of feedback stays colourful and informative instead of becoming a heat map of failure — and it maps directly onto what this card already distinguishes: a dropped Detail, a missed Read, a missing reaction, a recital.

I'll wait for the palette and accessibility jobs before committing to hexes rather than guess at them.

description: Replace the palette with the plum and category system
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu && python - <<'PY'
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old_head = s[:s.index('.rc {')]
new_head = '''/**
 * The Report Card's own world.
 *
 * Scoped entirely to `.rc`. Nothing here leaks, and the card borrows nothing
 * from the Activity Centre's cream surface or from the emerald that means
 * "carried" everywhere else in the wing. That is a decision, not drift
 * (Joshua, 2026-09-08): the cream-and-serif register reads as machine-written
 * now, emerald is spoken for, and a near-monochrome page is too austere for a
 * screen a student is meant to want to open.
 *
 * The scene it is built for: a student at nine in the evening, laptop open,
 * listening to her own voice against a recording of the same passage done
 * properly, working out what to fix before tomorrow. That forces two grounds.
 * The **console** is a deep plum field holding the verdict, the transport and
 * the ledger: the instrument, the parts she operates. The **page** is a
 * violet-cast white holding everything she reads at length.
 *
 * ── Why plum and violet ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
 *
 * Researched rather than picked (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). The hue families
 * currently saturated in award-winning work are acid yellow and chartreuse,
 * electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and terracotta, and black with
 * one neon accent. The under-used ones are violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty
 * rose used as a field, and complex cool neutrals. Emerald and green are the
 * owner's veto, cream is his veto, and orange-red was this file's first answer
 * and sits squarely in the over-used list. Violet and plum are what is left
 * that is both fresh and serious.
 *
 * The neutrals are tinted toward that violet the way Material 3's baseline is
 * (#fffbfe paper, #1c1b1f ink, #7d5260 plum tertiary), which is a system built
 * to hold WCAG AA at every step rather than a mood board.
 *
 * ── Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity ────────────────────
 *
 * The mechanic is Grammarly's: it colours by category, blue for clarity, green
 * for engagement, purple for tone, and reserves red for outright errors. That
 * is what keeps a page of feedback from becoming a heat map of failure. Here
 * the four are fixed and each means one thing everywhere on the card:
 *
 * - **Detail** (plum-rose) is what she was holding and what slipped out of it.
 * - **Read** (teal) is whether she found the move the argument turns on.
 * - **Colour** (gold) is what she brought of her own, and what she held.
 * - **Method** (indigo) is how she ran it: reciting, listing, going quiet.
 *
 * A miss is not red. It is the colour of the thing it is a miss OF, which is
 * also what lets her scan the page for one kind of problem at a time.
 *
 * **Signal** (magenta) is the exception and is not a category: it is sound. It
 * marks the line currently playing and nothing else, so the only saturated
 * colour that moves is the playhead.
 *
 * Band is still carried by FILL, not by hue, so the ledger survives grayscale
 * and a colour-blind reader: a solid mark held the lot, a hollow ring lost it.
 */

'''
s = new_head + s[s.index('.rc {'):]

# ---- tokens ----
old_tokens_start = s.index('.rc {')
old_tokens_end = s.index('  background: var(--rc-page);')
new_tokens = '''.rc {
  /* ── The console: a deep plum field ─────────────────────────────────── */
  --rc-console: oklch(0.245 0.058 312);
  --rc-console-raised: oklch(0.315 0.062 312);
  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.385 0.055 312);
  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.968 0.014 312);
  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);

  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

/* ── Category accents ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   One class per kind of feedback. The label takes the hue and a rule sits

.rc__movement {

}

/* A panel in the tone of the section it sits in. */
.rc__panel {
  background: var(--rc-tone-soft, var(--rc-fill));

}

a='''/** A section of the open stimulus. One label style, one rhythm, same order. */
function Movement({
  label,
  children,
  className,
}: {
  label: string;
  children: React.ReactNode;
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <section className={cn("border-t border-[var(--rc-hairline)] pt-5", className)}>
      <p className="rc__label">{label}</p>
      <div className="mt-3">{children}</div>
    </section>
  );
}'''
b='''/**
 * A section of the open stimulus. One label style, one rhythm, same order.
 *
 * `tone` is the kind of feedback the section carries, not how bad it is: a
 * dropped Detail and a held one are both `detail`, and the rule above the
 * label is the same colour for each. See the palette note in report-card.css
 * for why that is the whole point.
 */
function Movement({
  label,
  tone,
  children,
  className,
}: {
  label: string;
  tone?: "detail" | "read" | "colour" | "method";
  children: React.ReactNode;
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <section className={cn("rc__movement pt-5", tone && `rc__movement--${tone}`, className)}>
      <p className="rc__label">{label}</p>
      <div className="mt-3">{children}</div>
    </section>
  );
}'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)

# Assign a tone to every movement.
for lab,tone in [('label="What happened"','method'),('label="What you held"','colour'),
                 ('label="What slipped"','detail'),('label="Your Read"','read'),
                 ('label="Line by line"','detail'),('label="What you said"','method'),
                 ('label="Nothing to fix"','colour'),('label="Next time"','method')]:
    a=f'<Movement {lab}>'
    assert a in t, lab
    t=t.replace(a, f'<Movement {lab} tone="{tone}">')

# The Read's quote takes the read tone; the whole-thing box takes detail.
t=t.replace('''<p className="mt-3 rounded-lg bg-[var(--rc-fill)] px-4 py-3 text-[15px] italic leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                          The Read this one turns on: {s.the_read}''',
'''<p className="rc__panel mt-3 text-[15px] italic leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                          The Read this one turns on: {s.the_read}''')
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(t)
print('tsx ok')

command: python - <<'PY'
p='.impeccable/surfaces/src-features-activity-centre.md'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
i=s.index('## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)')
s=s[:i]+'''## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)

Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream, not
emerald, and not grey** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI
looking", "I don't want emerald", "it should be much more colorful"). The second
sanctioned departure from the wing's surface after the RRC door.

- **Two grounds.** A deep plum console (`oklch(0.245 0.058 312)`) holds the
  verdict, the transport and the ledger; a violet-cast white page
  (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`) holds everything she reads at length.
- **Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity.** The mechanic is
  Grammarly's, which colours by category and reserves red for outright errors.
  Four fixed tones: **Detail** plum-rose, **Read** teal, **Colour** gold,
  **Method** indigo. A miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of, which is
  what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure.
- **Signal magenta is sound, not a category.** It marks the line currently
  playing and nothing else, held at hue 328 so it separates from the
  plum-rose dropped chips it shares a line with.
- **Hues chosen from research, not taste** (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). Saturated
  right now: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and
  terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft
  cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. Green and cream are the
  owner's veto; orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used
  list. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is
  (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary), a system built to hold AA.
- **Band is fill, not hue.** The ledger's marks differ by how full they are, in
  gold; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow rose ring. It survives
  grayscale, which is what lets category own the colour.
- **One stimulus at a time.** Twenty-two accordion rows became a ledger of
  twenty-two marks plus one open stimulus in a fixed order: the passage, what
  you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.
- **No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and she has
  never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says
  "Almost all of it came back" instead (Joshua, 2026-09-08).
- **Contrast is measured, not assumed.** `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs` converts
  computed colours through a canvas (Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`) and
  every text node clears 4.5:1 on its own ground, in the default, playing and
  clean states.
- **Type ramp:** Fraunces display, Lexend body, IBM Plex Mono labels. The design
  detector reports the sizes as outside DESIGN.md; that is this sanctioned world,
  not drift, the same exception the RRC door's `--rd-*` tokens carry.
- **Harness:** `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders it at twenty-two
  stimuli without `App.tsx`. `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to
  freeze the playhead on a line. `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs` captures the matrix.
'''
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('ok')

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit the report card rebuild
command: git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
The Report Card is a page she can read, in colour that means something

Twenty-two collapsible rows, each opening into the same ten stacked blocks of
undifferentiated small text, was a list to be got through rather than a report.
It is now a ledger of twenty-two marks on a console plus exactly one stimulus
open below it, in a fixed order she learns once: the passage, what you held,
what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.

The transcript follows the sound. The grader already stored a start and an end
for every line she spoke, so the playhead alone is enough to walk a highlight
down her run while it plays. No word timings, no re-grade, and old grades get it
too. Her recording and the standard's sit in one transport instead of two text
links at the bottom of an expanded row.

Praise is specific or it is noise, and there was none: a stimulus at the
standard got one sentence and one at 86% got nothing, so twenty of twenty-two
rows were a list of what went wrong. "What you held" is read off measured facts
and sits ahead of the correction.

No Detail arithmetic on screen. A Detail is our grading unit, it is what the
judge is asked to mark, and a student has never seen one enumerated: she read a
passage and said it back. "20 of 21 details" hands her our machinery and asks
her to care about the one number on the page she cannot check. It says "Almost
all of it came back" now.

Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity, which is the mechanic that
stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure. Detail
is plum-rose, the Read is teal, what she brought of her own is gold, method is
indigo, and a miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of. Magenta is the
exception and means sound: the line playing, and nothing else. Band stays
carried by fill rather than hue, so the ledger survives grayscale.

The hues were researched rather than picked. Orange-red was this file's first
answer and sits in the over-used list beside acid yellow, electric cobalt and
black-plus-one-neon; violet, plum and soft cyan are under-used. Cream and
emerald are the owner's veto. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way
Material 3's baseline is, a system built to hold AA rather than a mood board.

playing and clean states. The two faint inks sit where they do because that is
where they pass; there is no large-text exemption at 10px.

## What the research changed

The Firecrawl jobs indicted two of my own choices. The hue families currently saturated in award-winning work are acid yellow, electric cobalt, **orange and coral**, warm sand and terracotta, and **black plus one neon accent** — which is exactly what I'd built. Under-used: violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. With green and cream vetoed by you, violet and plum are what's left that's both fresh and serious.

The single most useful finding was Grammarly's mechanic: it colours by **category** (blue for clarity, green for engagement, purple for tone) and reserves red for outright errors. That's what keeps a page of feedback from becoming a heat map of failure — and it maps onto what this card already distinguishes.

So the four tones are now fixed and each means a *kind* of feedback, never a severity:

- **Detail** plum-rose — what she was holding, and what slipped out of it
- **Read** teal — whether she found the move the argument turns on
- **Colour** gold — what she brought of her own, and what she held
- **Method** indigo — reciting, listing, going quiet

A miss is the colour of the thing it's a miss *of*. Magenta is the one exception and means sound: the line playing, nothing else. Band stays carried by fill rather than hue, so the ledger still survives grayscale.

Neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary) — a system built to hold AA rather than a mood board.

## Detail counts are gone

"20 of 21 details" is out everywhere. A Detail is our grading unit; she's never seen one enumerated. It reads "Almost all of it came back" now, and the console says "of what was on the page, said back in your own words".

## Verified

96 test files, 2,685 tests, typecheck clean. Every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser across the default, playing and clean states — 34 samples, zero failures. Screenshots at 1180px and 390px.

That's what was 500ing the dev server earlier. safe-push refuses to merge over it, and I won't stash or discard someone else's in-flight work.

Tell me to drop that local change and I'll push immediately; upstream appears to have already fixed the same file.

Two Firecrawl jobs are still running (education results screens, and the best non-green colour for "correct"). They may sharpen the gold, but nothing is waiting on them.

oose the blue family for a correct-answer state, using a darker accessible blue in production rather than pure #0000FF. Blue is the best compromise when green is unavailable: it is familiar, calm, color-vision-friendly, and visually distinct from the red/orange error family, while being less warning-like than amber/coral and less culturally or semantically loaded than violet, plum, gold, or pink. The main ambiguity—blue meaning link, focus, selection, or information—must be removed with a checkmark, explicit ‘Correct’ text, a distinct background/border treatment, and non-color cues. If the product already uses blue for primary actions or selection, use teal as the fallback, but test it because teal can still collapse into green.

## What the last two research jobs said

**On colour:** the recommendation was blue for a "correct" state, teal as fallback, with an explicit warning that gold "can mean premium, money, rarity, or rank rather than simply correct." That reads like it indicts the gold, but it doesn't apply here — that advice is for a binary correct/incorrect token, and this card deliberately has no such token. Gold isn't "correct" on the Report Card, it's the *Colour* category: what she brought of her own. In the ledger, where gold fill does encode band, "achievement or rank" is the right reading. The one thing worth checking was the warning that teal can collapse into green given your emerald veto, and it doesn't: my Read teal sits at hue 218, which is blue-cyan. Green is around 150. **No rework.**

**On education results screens:** one real defect, now fixed. Every product that does spoken feedback well surfaces a small actionable set rather than every imperfection, and Busuu names three. The grader has been returning exactly that in `bigMisses` since it was written, and the card rendered it as a single line of grey text at the foot of the line-by-line block, under every chip and panel. It now leads "What slipped", with the full list of missed Details folded underneath.

## What I'd do next, in order

1. **Look at it as Celia.** Everything I've verified is against a fixture. The card has never been seen with a real recording behind it, so the one thing genuinely unproven is the playhead: whether the stored line timings actually land where her voice is. That needs a student login, which I don't have.
2. **The retry path.** The education research was consistent that a miss should come with a way to try again. The console ends on a sentence about what to do tomorrow and a Done button. There's a redrill test in the runner, so something may exist to wire up.
3. **The `next_time` voice pass.** Still outstanding from this morning: 11 em-dashes across 44 lines, "Push past just retelling" ten times. That's the judge's wording, not the card's, and it needs a prompt change plus a deploy.

Loose end: `.claude/launch.json` still has my unused `rrc-report` entry uncommitted. Say the word and I'll revert it.
