## 2026-09-08 (09:30 UTC) — The Report Card becomes a page she can read, in colour that means something

Joshua's brief, in his words: highly organised, easy to follow and run through, high-end, click to hear her recording with the transcript highlighting as it plays against the golden standard, very specific critiques and very specific congratulations, spoken in his voice. Then three corrections as it went: **not cream** ("overdone and too AI looking"), **not emerald**, and **much more colourful than grey**. Green was a typo, not a request.

**Structure.** Twenty-two collapsible rows, each opening into ten stacked blocks of undifferentiated small text, became a ledger of twenty-two marks on a console plus exactly one stimulus open below it, in a fixed order she learns once: the passage, what you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time. Previous/Next walks the run without going back to the ledger.

**The transcript follows the sound.** The grader already stored `startMs`/`endMs` for every line she spoke, so the playhead alone drives the highlight; no word timings, no re-grade, and grades written before today get it too. Her recording and the standard's are one transport at the top rather than two text links at the foot of an expanded row. Line timings exist on 68 of 74 lines in Celia's row, and the six without simply get no play button.

**Specific congratulation.** "What you held" is read off measured facts and sits ahead of the correction. Before this a stimulus at the standard got one sentence and one at 86% got nothing, so twenty of twenty-two rows were a list of what went wrong.

**No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and a student has never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says "Almost all of it came back". Joshua's rule, and it applies to any future student-facing surface.

**Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity** — the mechanic is Grammarly's, and it is what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure. Detail plum-rose, Read teal, what she brought of her own gold, method indigo; a miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of. Magenta is the exception and means sound. Band stays carried by FILL not hue, so the ledger survives grayscale.

**Hues researched, not picked** (Firecrawl agents, server-side). Over-used in current award-winning work: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty rose as a field, cool neutrals. Orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used list, so it moved. Neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is (`#fffbfe`, `#7d5260`), a system built to hold AA. A later job recommended blue for a generic "correct" token and warned gold reads as rank; it does not apply, because this card has no correct/incorrect token and gold is a category, not a verdict.

**`bigMisses` promoted.** The grader has returned at most three plain-words misses since it was written and the card printed them as one grey line at the foot of the line-by-line block. They now lead "What slipped", with the full missed list folded under. Every spoken-feedback product that does this well surfaces a small actionable set (Busuu names three).

**Verified:** 96 test files / 2,685 Activity Centre tests, typecheck clean, and every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser across the default, playing and clean states (34 samples, 0 failures) via `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs`, which converts computed colours through a canvas because Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`. Screenshots at 1180px and 390px via `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs`.

**New for future RRC report work:** `src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css` is the scoped world (`.rc`), the design decisions are written into its docstring and into `.impeccable/surfaces/src-features-activity-centre.md` under "The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)". `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders the card at twenty-two stimuli **without `App.tsx`**, which is what made it workable while another session's CSS was mid-edit and 500ing the dev server: `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to freeze the playhead.

Commits `3bd7aba`, `a9cb6e2`, both on origin/main. Discarded with Joshua's confirmation: an uncommitted local edit to `questionfinder/questionFinder.css` from another session; origin's `c8e4478` already carried the better fix (it hides the shortcut hint too).

**Correction to an earlier claim in this session:** I reported the "Every cylinder" branch as dead code because the judge never returns an empty `next_time` across 44 real stimuli. That was wrong. The card gates on measured criteria, not the empty string, so it fires correctly; Celia never triggers it because she gave no Read on any of the 22, which is the right answer. The narrower finding stands: the judge has never once left `next_time` empty.

Not done: no student-login browser check, so the playhead has never been seen against a real recording. No retry path from a miss, which the education research was consistent about. The `next_time` voice pass is still outstanding (11 em-dashes across 44 lines, "Push past just retelling" ten times) and needs a prompt change plus a deploy.
EOF

command: python - <<'PY'
p='src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css'
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
s += '''
/* ── The ground the card lands on ─────────────────────────────────────────
   The runner's report phase is a `.activity-centre` page, which paints the
   Centre's warm cream (`38 36% 93%`). A violet card floating on warm cream is
   the exact clash the palette above was chosen to get away from, and the
   student never sees the card on the neutral ground it was designed against.
   So the report phase brings its own ground, tuned one step darker than the
   card's page so the card still reads as a card. */
.rc-ground {
  background: oklch(0.925 0.014 312);
}

/* ── The focus ring ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   `.activity-centre :focus-visible` forces an emerald ring on every focusable
   thing inside the module. Emerald is precisely what this card must not have,
   and it was reaching the ledger, the transport and every "Hear it" button.
   Same shape as the module's ring, in the colour that means sound here. */
.activity-centre .rc :focus-visible,
.rc :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-page),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal);
  outline: none;
}

/* On the console the ring has to clear a dark plum instead of paper. */
.activity-centre .rc__console :focus-visible,
.rc__console :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-console),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal-bright);
}
'''

The card lands on its own ground, and stops wearing an emerald focus ring

Verified in a harness on a neutral field, which is not where a student ever
sees it. The runner's report phase is a `.activity-centre` page and that class
paints the Centre's warm cream, so the plum card she gets at the end of a drill

the student are looking at the same page.

the module, and it was reaching the ledger, the transport and every "Hear it"
button on a card whose whole brief was no emerald. Same ring shape, in the
colour that means sound here, with a variant that clears the dark plum console.

Both overrides are compounded onto `.activity-centre` rather than left bare:
they tie it on specificity otherwise, which decides the winner on stylesheet
order, and that is not a thing to leave to chance across a code-split build.

`?frame=runner` on the preview harness now wraps the card in the exact element
the runner uses, cream and emerald ring and all. Without it the harness renders
a page no student sees, which is how this got as far as it did.

## assistant

Pushed as `7939bd0`. Asking that question caught two real defects that my harness was hiding.

**What she actually gets after finishing the drill**

I traced the path: `ActivityRunner` phase `report` → `SectionReport` → `SectionReportCard`. Both render sites import the same component, so there's no stale second copy of the card anywhere — the admin attempt view (`ActivityResults`) gets the rebuild too.

**Two things were broken on that path, and only on that path**

1. **The card was floating on warm cream.** The report phase is wrapped in `.activity-centre`, and that class paints `hsl(38 36% 93%)`. So the plum card a student sees at the end of a drill was sitting on exactly the surface you told me to get away from. I'd verified everything on a neutral harness field no student ever sees. The phase now brings its own ground, one step darker than the card's page so the card still reads as a card.

2. **Every focusable thing in the card wore an emerald ring.** `.activity-centre :focus-visible` forces emerald on everything inside the module — the ledger ticks, the transport, every "Hear it" button — on the one card whose brief was no emerald. Measured it before and after: the ring is now plum and magenta, `ringHasEmerald: false`.

Both overrides are compounded onto `.activity-centre` rather than left bare, because a lone `.rc-ground` ties it on specificity and the winner would then depend on stylesheet order across a code-split build.

**Verified**

96 files / 2,685 tests, typecheck clean, production build succeeds in 44s, and I confirmed `rc-ground{background:#e9e4ed}` and the console focus rule are both present in the shipped `report-card-B3QMfZle.css`. Contrast re-measured inside the real wrapper: 29 samples, zero failures.

The harness now has `?frame=runner`, which wraps the card in the exact element the runner uses. That's the mode I should have been checking against from the start.

**Still true**

The playhead has never run against a real recording — no student login. Everything else about the completion path is now verified against the real frame rather than a convenient one.

   *
   * Its own Report Card is what opens: a completed section routes there rather
   * than to the generic summary, from any device and at any later date.

  onOpenAttempt?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /** Injected in tests. */
  loadChoices?: () => Promise<SectionChoice[]>;

  beginAttempt?: (activityId: string) => Promise<string>;
}

function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
}

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.
 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.
 */
function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}

export function SectionLibrary({
  attempts,

  onOpenAttempt,

}: SectionLibraryProps) {

A past Attempt says what it opens, because Celia could not find her Report Card

Her words, the morning after her first graded section: "I see the percentage,
but I'm not seeing a way to look back at the attempt to see what information I
was missing in my responses."

Nothing was broken. The routing has been right for weeks: a completed section
reopens into its Report Card at the assignment's own address, and this row was
already a button that goes there. The row just never said so. It was 13.5px
muted text with a dotted underline, no border and no padding, which is what a
caption looks like, and the label stopped at the number. She read the score off
it and reasonably concluded that was all there was.

So the row is a control now: bordered, full width, 44px of target, and it names
its destination. "Attempt 2 · 2 Sept · 91% carried · See what you missed". A run
still being marked says "Check back shortly" instead of going quiet, and an
unfinished one stays flat and dashed with no action at all.

A percentage is a verdict she cannot act on. The card behind it is the whole
point of the drill.

Found by the student, not by the suite, so it gets a test.

## assistant

Fixed and pushed (`01f3621`). Celia's report was accurate, and the diagnosis is a bit humbling.

**Nothing was actually broken.** The routing has been correct for weeks: her attempt `3afbbbde` is `completed` / `section` / `spoken-recall`, PT1.S3 is a listed section, and `/activities/:id` reopens a completed section straight into its Report Card. The button she needed was already there and already pointed at the right place.

**It just didn't look like a button.** The attempt row was 13.5px muted text with a dotted underline, no border, no padding — visually a caption. And the label stopped at the number: `Attempt 1 · 8 Sep · 87% carried`. She read the score off it and reasonably concluded that was all there was. Her sentence is almost a description of the CSS.

So the row is a control now: bordered, full width, 44px target, and it names where it goes.

> Attempt 2 · 2 Sept · 91% carried — **See what you missed**

A run still being marked says "Check back shortly" rather than going silent. An unfinished one stays flat and dashed with no action.

2,686 tests pass, typecheck clean. It got a regression test, because a student found this and the suite didn't.

**One gap I did not close, and you should know about it**

`useClassroomHome.ts:184` filters `row.status !== "completed"`, so the moment she finishes a section its row disappears from Classroom. That's right for a to-do list, but an RRC Report Card is produced *minutes after* she stops — completed is exactly when it becomes worth opening. Her only route is now Classroom → the section-library row → PT1.S3 → the attempt row. Three levels, with nothing telling her a card exists.

The complete fix is a "your Report Card is ready" row in Classroom when a grade lands. That's a more invasive change to the feed, so I didn't do it unasked — but it's the difference between her being able to find it and her being told it's there.

**Worth replying to her now:** her card is live at PT1.S3 in the section library, and she's at 87% with 17 of 22 at the standard. Want me to draft that?

  const markDone = React.useCallback(

      setCompletingKey(key);

b='''  /*
   * Her Report Cards, while they are still news.
   *
   * Grading finishes minutes after she stops, by which point the run is over
   * and its row has left this page: `workRows` above drops anything completed,
   * which is right for a list of things still to do and is exactly why a
   * finished section had nowhere to announce itself. Celia sat one, saw a
   * percentage somewhere else, and had to ask where the graded responses were.
   *
   * So a graded card gets a row of its own until she opens it, and then it
   * stops: "Assigned to you" is work outstanding, and a card she has read is
   * not outstanding. The Section Library keeps every Attempt for ever, which is
   * where it lives afterwards.
   */
  const gradesQuery = useQuery({
    queryKey: ["classroom-home:section-grades", user?.id],
    enabled: !!user && !permissions.is_admin,
    queryFn: () => listMySectionGrades(),
  });

  const reportRows = React.useMemo<HomeRow[]>(() => {
    if (permissions.is_admin || !access.activities) return [];
    return (gradesQuery.data ?? [])
      .filter((g) =>
        shouldPromptReportCard(g.graded_at, g.assignment_id, g.attempt_no, now.getTime()),
      )
      .sort((x, y) => new Date(y.graded_at).getTime() - new Date(x.graded_at).getTime())
      .map((g) => ({
        key: `report:${g.assignment_id}:${g.attempt_no}`,
        title: "Your Report Card is ready",
        kind: "activity" as HomeKind,
        tone: KIND_TONE.activity,
        meta: ["Read, Recall, Colour", g.source_section, assignedLabel(g.graded_at, now)]
          .filter(Boolean)
          .join(" \\u00b7 "),
        route: `/activities/${g.assignment_id}`,
        // Not "Open". She has already done the work; what this offers is the

        actionLabel: "See what you missed",
        emphasis: false,
        isNew: true,
        dueToday: false,
        overdue: false,
        // Nothing for her to tick off. Opening it is what closes it.
        canComplete: false,
      }));
  }, [gradesQuery.data, permissions.is_admin, access.activities, now]);

  const readingRows = React.useMemo<HomeRow[]>(() => {'''

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
   * matter. The order is the page's argument: the top row is what to do next.
   */
  const rows = React.useMemo(() => {
    const ranked = [...workRows].sort((a, b) => {
      const rank = (row: HomeRow) => (row.overdue ? 0 : row.dueToday ? 1 : 2);
      const delta = rank(a) - rank(b);
      if (delta !== 0) return delta;
      return a.isNew === b.isNew ? 0 : a.isNew ? -1 : 1;
    });
    // Unread reading matter goes above ordinary work but below anything
    // overdue or due today. Appending it after every assignment looked right
    // and was not: Home shows four rows, so a student with four open
    // assignments never saw a new note at all, which is the case this whole
    // feature exists for.
    const urgent = ranked.filter((row) => row.overdue || row.dueToday);
    const rest = ranked.filter((row) => !(row.overdue || row.dueToday));
    const unreadReading = readingRows.filter((row) => row.isNew);
    const readReading = readingRows.filter((row) => !row.isNew);
    const all = [...urgent, ...unreadReading, ...rest, ...readReading];
    if (all.length) all[0] = { ...all[0], emphasis: true };
    return all;
  }, [readingRows, workRows]);

  // The hero takes the top row. The list below repeats it deliberately: the
  // hero is the argument for doing it now, the list is the inventory, and a
  // student scanning only the list should still find the thing that is due.
  const heroRow = rows[0] ?? null;
  const listRows = rows.slice(0, 4);
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit and push the home page work
command: git add src/components/classroom/home/reportCardSeen.ts src/components/classroom/home/reportCardSeen.test.ts src/components/classroom/home/useClassroomHome.ts src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReport.test.tsx && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
A ready Report Card reaches her home page, and leaves it once she opens it

Grading finishes minutes after she stops, and by then the run is completed, so
`workRows` has dropped it: "Assigned to you" is a list of work outstanding and
that filter is right. It also meant a finished section had nowhere on the site
to announce itself, which is why Celia had to ask where her graded responses
were.

A graded card now gets a row of its own, above ordinary work and below anything
overdue. Only four rows are drawn, so anything appended to the end is invisible
to a student with a full week, and this is news with a short life. It never
takes the hero: the hero argues for doing something next and labels its button
from the kind of work it is, which would offer a finished run back to her as
"Start".

Opening it is what closes it. There is no Done control, because a card she has
read is not outstanding work and ticking it off is not a thing she should have
to do. The Section Library keeps the Attempt afterwards, for ever.

Two independent reasons it leaves, so a cleared browser cannot make one linger:
she opened it, or it went stale at three days. The flag is in `localStorage`
because `activity_assignments` has no column for it and production schema
changes here do not go through a migration file in this repo. The cost is
honest: on a second device she is nudged once more and then it settles, and
nothing about her work depends on it.

Also wraps `SectionReport`'s tests in a Router. It calls `useNavigate` since the
focus-set path landed, and its tests were rendering it bare.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
bash .claude/safe-push.sh 2>&1 | tail -5
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## What's happening

Ivory's account has **`has_activity_access: false`**. He's the only one of the nine students with RRC assigned who is missing that flag:

| Student | activity | assignments |
|---|---|---|
| Steven, Fernande, Sofia, Celia, Yahunathan, Charles, Ore | true | 4–9 each |
| **Ivory Johnson** | **false** | 3 |

He does have `has_classroom_access: true`, which is why he can sign in and reach the classroom — and why the failure looks like a broken link rather than a locked account.

That one flag explains **both** of his emails:

1. **Yesterday** ("I don't see the assigned work on my portal"). Classroom home filters activity rows out for anyone without the flag — `useClassroomHome.ts:189`. His three RRC assignments were invisible to him.
2. **Today** (the screenshot). `/activities/:id` sits behind `ProtectedRoute flag="has_activity_access"`, so it renders "Not open yet — This part of the classroom isn't in your plan right now." That's our own gate doing exactly what it was told.

He has three RRC sections assigned, all created today, all `status=assigned`: PT1.S3, PT21.S3, PT101.S2.

Flip `has_activity_access` to `true` on his profile (`class_id 59790b57-7bd9-46a2-bab2-35c606f4ecb2`). One field. It's toggleable from the Admin dashboard's per-feature controls, or I can run the update.

// One definition of "a paying student's access", used by the Stripe webhook and
// anything else that needs to open the product after a payment.
//
// A new account starts locked (see the profiles column defaults): they can see
// their classroom, finish the intake, message Joshua and book. Paying turns on
// the rest. Nothing here ever revokes — a refund is Joshua's call from People,
// not something a webhook should decide.

export const PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS = [
  "has_classroom_access",
  "has_practice_access",
  "has_drill_access",
  "has_bootcamp_access",
  "has_journal_access",
  "has_waj_access",
  "has_chat_access",
  "has_schedule_access",
  "has_activity_access",
] as const;

export type GrantResult =
  | { granted: true; class_id: string }
  | { granted: false; reason: "no_email" | "no_account" };

/** Opens the paid feature set for whoever owns `email`. */
export async function grantStudentAccess(
  admin: { auth: { admin: { listUsers: (opts: { perPage: number }) => Promise<any> } }; from: (t: string) => any },
  email: string | null | undefined,
): Promise<GrantResult> {
  if (!email) return { granted: false, reason: "no_email" };
  const target = email.trim().toLowerCase();

  const { data } = await admin.auth.admin.listUsers({ perPage: 1000 });
  const user = (data?.users ?? []).find(
    (u: { email?: string | null }) => (u.email ?? "").toLowerCase() === target,
  );
  // Someone can pay before they ever make an account. That is not an error:
  // Joshua grants them by hand once they sign up, and the payment record still
  // exists either way.
  if (!user) return { granted: false, reason: "no_account" };

  const updates: Record<string, boolean> = {};
  for (const flag of PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS) updates[flag] = true;

  const { error } = await admin.from("profiles").update(updates).eq("class_id", user.id);
[/external_agent_tool_result]

// -------------------------------------------------------------- resolution

interface StudentRow {
  class_id: string;
  display_name: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  [k: string]: unknown;
}

/**
 * Students come from the admin-manage-users edge function, which cross-joins
 * profiles with auth.users for the email. `class_id` is the canonical student
 * identifier everywhere in this codebase.
 */
async function listStudents(): Promise<StudentRow[]> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);
  const rows = (data ?? []) as StudentRow[];
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) throw new Error("admin-manage-users returned no user list");
  return rows;
}

/**
 * Resolve a name or email to exactly one student. Ambiguity is fatal rather
 * than resolved by picking the first: sending a student's homework to the wrong
 * account is silent — she simply never sees it, and he never learns why.
 */
function resolveStudent(rows: StudentRow[], query: string): StudentRow {
  const q = query.trim().toLowerCase();
  const exact = rows.filter(
    (r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q,
  );
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows.filter(
    (r) =>
      r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q),
  );

  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no student matches "${query}".`);
    console.error("Known:", rows.map((r) => r.display_name ?? r.email).join(" · "));
    process.exit(1);
  }

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Add an access guard to the assign path
command: python - <<'PY'
p='scripts/assign.ts'
t=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

helper = '''
// ------------------------------------------------------------ access

/**
 * The flag that has to be on for a student to open what she was just assigned.
 *
 * Every route into this work sits behind a `ProtectedRoute`, and Classroom's
 * own feed drops rows a student cannot open, so a missing flag does not read as
 * a locked door: the work is simply invisible, and the link Joshua sends says
 * "Not open yet".
 *
 * That happened. Ivory Johnson was assigned three Read, Recall, Colour sections
 * on 2026-09-08 with `has_activity_access` off. He mailed Joshua on the Monday
 * to say the portal was empty, tried the link on the Tuesday, was told it was
 * not in his plan, and lost a day of study to a boolean. Nothing in the assign
 * path had any opinion about whether he could reach what it was writing.
 *
 * Assigning work he cannot open is never what Joshua means, so this grants the
 * one flag the assigned work needs. It never grants anything else and it never
 * revokes: the rest of the feature set stays exactly where he set it.
 */
const ACCESS_FLAG = {
  activity: "has_activity_access",
  guided_drill: "has_drill_access",
} as const;

async function ensureCanOpen(classId: string, kind: keyof typeof ACCESS_FLAG, who: string) {
  const flag = ACCESS_FLAG[kind];
  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from("profiles")
    .select(flag)
    .eq("class_id", classId)
    .single();
  if (error) {
    console.warn(`! Could not check ${flag} for ${who}: ${error.message}`);
    console.warn("  The work is assigned. Confirm they can open it before telling them it is there.");
    return;
  }
  if ((data as Record<string, boolean>)?.[flag]) return;

  const { error: upErr } = await supabase
    .from("profiles")
    .update({ [flag]: true })
    .eq("class_id", classId);
  if (upErr) {
    console.error(`! ${who} does not have ${flag} and it could not be turned on: ${upErr.message}`);
    console.error("  They will see \\"Not open yet\\". Turn it on in Admin before telling them.");
    return;
  }
  console.log(`  ${flag} was off for ${who}. Turned it on, or they could not have opened this.`);
}

'''

a = '''async function main() {'''
assert a in t
t = t.replace(a, helper.lstrip("\n") + a, 1)

a = '''  const r = ok[0];
  const n = r.notify;'''
b = '''  const r = ok[0];
  const n = r.notify;
  // After the write, not before: a student who is never assigned anything has
  // no business having a feature opened for them.
  await ensureCanOpen(student.class_id, item.kind, who);'''
assert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(t)
print('ok')
PY

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Commit and push the systemic fix
command: git add scripts/assign.ts scripts/access-audit.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
Assigning work turns on the flag needed to open it, because Ivory lost a day

A missing feature flag does not look like a locked door from the student's
side. Classroom's feed drops rows the student cannot open, so the work is
simply not there, and the direct link answers "Not open yet. This part of the
classroom isn't in your plan right now."

Ivory Johnson was assigned three Read, Recall, Colour sections on 2026-09-08
with `has_activity_access` off. He mailed Joshua on the Monday to say the portal
was empty, tried the link on the Tuesday, got the gate, and lost a day of study
to a boolean. Every other student with RRC had the flag; his was a hand-granted
subset that missed one. Nothing in the assign path had any opinion about whether
he could reach what it was writing.

So `assign.ts` now grants the one flag the assigned work needs, after the write
and never before: a student who is never assigned anything has no business
having a feature opened for them. It grants nothing else and revokes nothing,
so the rest of the feature set stays where Joshua set it. If the check or the
update fails it says so loudly rather than reporting a clean assign.

`scripts/access-audit.ts` is the sweep for everything that arrives by other
paths: the admin UI, a Lovable-side write, a hand-run SQL. It is read-only and
names what is wrong without fixing it, because opening a feature on a student's
account is Joshua's call. Currently clean: 13 students with assigned work, all
of them able to open it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
bash .claude/safe-push.sh 2>&1 | tail -4
[/external_agent_tool_call]

command: ls -la wiki/IVORY-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md 2>/dev/null || cp /tmp/ivory_reply.txt wiki/IVORY-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md && echo "vault copy saved"; cat >> wiki/log.md <<'EOF'

## 2026-09-08 (11:20 UTC) — Ivory could not open his drill, and nothing was watching

Two mails from Ivory Johnson III. Monday: "would you know when I should be able to see the assigned work on my portal?" Tuesday, with a screenshot: "Not open yet. This part of the classroom isn't in your plan right now."

**Not a broken link.** `has_activity_access` was `false` on his profile while `has_classroom_access` was `true`, which is why he could sign in and reach the classroom and it looked like the URL was at fault. One flag caused both mails: `/activities/:id` sits behind `ProtectedRoute flag="has_activity_access"`, and Classroom's own feed drops activity rows for anyone without it (`useClassroomHome.ts:189`), so his three RRC sections were invisible AND unreachable. He is the only one of nine students with RRC assigned who was missing it; his was a hand-granted subset (classroom, drill, chat, schedule) that missed activity. Turned it on at Joshua's go-ahead. There is no per-drill flag and there cannot be one, but the scoping was moot: `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments and he has zero non-RRC activities, so the flag opened exactly the three RRC sections and nothing else.

**The systemic fix.** `scripts/assign.ts` now grants the flag the assigned work needs, after the write, never before, granting nothing else and revoking nothing; it fails loudly rather than reporting a clean assign. `scripts/access-audit.ts` is the read-only sweep for work arriving by other paths (admin UI, Lovable-side writes, hand-run SQL) and names problems without fixing them, because opening a feature on a student's account is Joshua's call. Currently clean: 13 students with assigned work, all able to open it. Commit `15a5a2b`.

**Reply drafted, not sent:** Gmail draft in thread `1a07c3c954127a8c`, copy at `wiki/IVORY-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md`. Owns it in the first line, names the cause, tells him the three sections and to re-sign-in if the page is stale.

**The lesson, which is the point of writing this down.** The product had no opinion about whether a student could open what it had just written to their account. Every route is gated and every feed filters, so a missing flag is not a locked door, it is an empty page: the failure is silent on both sides, and the only detector was a student polite enough to send a screenshot. When a surface is gated AND its feed filters on the same flag, assigning into it needs a guarantee at the write, not a hope. Next time work is assigned by any path, check the student can reach it in the same breath.

All four done.

## The reply

Drafted in Gmail, in his existing thread, correctly threaded with `In-Reply-To`. **Not sent** — it's sitting as a draft for you to review. Copy also in the vault.

It opens by owning it in the first line, names the cause without jargon, tells him the three sections by name, and flags the sign-out-and-back-in in case his browser is holding stale permissions. 195 words, no em-dashes, no banned phrases.

## It works

Verified rather than assumed:
- `has_activity_access` read back as `true` on his profile
- `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments, and he has **0** non-RRC activities, so the flag opened exactly the three RRC sections and nothing else
- His other flags (`practice`, `bootcamp`, `ta`) are untouched and still `false`

## It can't happen to anyone else

Two layers, pushed as `15a5a2b`:

**`scripts/assign.ts` now grants the flag the assigned work needs** — after the write, never before, since a student who's never assigned anything has no business having a feature opened. It grants nothing else, revokes nothing, and fails loudly rather than reporting a clean assign.

**`scripts/access-audit.ts`** is the read-only sweep for work arriving by other paths — admin UI, Lovable-side writes, hand-run SQL. It names problems without fixing them, because opening a feature on a student's account is your call. Ran it: **clean, 13 students with assigned work, all able to open it.**

## Logged, and what I'd do differently

Vault entry in `wiki/log.md`, plus a memory so it carries into future sessions.

The lesson worth keeping: **the product had no opinion about whether a student could open what it had just written to their account.** Every route is gated and every feed filters on the same flag, so a missing flag isn't a locked door — it's an empty page. The failure is silent on both sides: you see a successful assign, he sees nothing. The only detector was a student polite enough to send a screenshot.

Where I'd have done better: I've spent this session on the Report Card's *contents* while the path to it was the thing actually breaking for students. Celia couldn't find her card because a button looked like a caption; Ivory couldn't open his drill because of a boolean. Both were reachability, not design. Worth checking the route before polishing what's at the end of it.

# Students

One folder per student, holding **everything** about them (D2, locked 2026-07-16): transcripts, correspondence, homework, notes, schedules, profile. Open the student's folder and their whole working record is there. This is one of the brain's three knowledge systems, beside [[../wiki/index|wiki]] and [[../corpus/index|corpus]].

## Current students

- [[bella-lorenzo/bella-lorenzo|Bella Lorenzo]] · [[c-alexander/c-alexander|C. Alexander]] · [[dakota/dakota|Dakota]] · [[david-salgado/david-salgado|David Salgado]] · [[ethan/ethan|Ethan]] · [[favour/favour|Favour]] · [[lexi/lexi|Lexi A]] · [[lydia/lydia|Lydia]] · [[meghan/meghan|Meghan]] · [[nicole/nicole|Nicole]] · [[sofia/sofia|Sofia]] · [[yahunatan-zetvy/yahunatan-zetvy|Yahunatan Zetvy]]

## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)

Every file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:

- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**
- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.

**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).

## Standard files per student folder

- `<student-slug>.md` — compact student index and routing page (primary).
- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.
- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student's confirmed address.
- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.
- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory.
- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).
- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).
- `live-status.generated.md` — refreshable operational summary from Calendar, Gmail, payments, and LSAT U (generated mirror).
- `transcript-downloads/` — full local transcript exports (primary — the brain is their only home).
- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.

topic-map.md
transcript.md

# Student name aliases

Maps the names that show up in transcript filenames, calendar invites, and email
to the canonical student folder slug in `students/`.

It exists because Gemini names its exports from the meeting title, which is
whatever was typed at booking time: `Sofia`, `sofia`, `Sofia Minassian`,
`LSAT Session between Joshua and Meghan Mullins` all mean one student. Without
this file every pass re-derives the same mapping by hand and gets it slightly
differently.

Read by `system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py`. Format is one bullet per
alias, `alias -> folder-slug`. Matching is case- and punctuation-insensitive,
so only add entries the automatic matcher cannot get on its own (exact slug,
first name, and unambiguous containment are already handled).

## Aliases

- Adriana -> adriana-varea
- Adriana Varea -> adriana-varea
- Isabella Lorenzo -> bella-lorenzo
- Kaden Forges -> kaden-forges
- Thaniuska Vivas -> thaniuska-vivas
- Yahunathan -> yahunatan-zetvy
- YahuNathan -> yahunatan-zetvy
- Yahunatan -> yahunatan-zetvy

# Student roster

**Generated file — do not hand-edit.** Rebuild with:

```
python3 system/scripts/build-roster.py
```

Machine-readable twin: `students/roster.generated.json`, written by the
same run from the same rows. The command center's student picker reads
it through `/api/students`. A folder with no `<slug>.md` page is not a
student and appears in neither file.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

 "adrianavarea9@gmail.com":("adriana-varea","Adriana Varea"),
 "ynproemail@gmail.com":("yahunatan-zetvy","Yahunathan Zetvy"),

 "whitwilson929@gmail.com":("whit-wilson","Whit Wilson"),
 "moorecamooreca1@gmail.com":("charles-moore","Charles Moore"),
}
CONSULT={"akyrabrown@gmail.com":"Kyra Brown","ivoryj.johnson3@gmail.com":"Ivory Johnson",
 "amhuynh@asu.edu":"Amanda","cameronstribling@outlook.com":"Cameron Stribling","yoojulian7@gmail.com":"Julian Yoo"}
plan=[];consults=[];unknown=[]
for e in d.get('items',[]):
    att=e.get('attachments') or []
    if not att: continue
    date=(e.get('start',{}).get('dateTime') or e.get('start',{}).get('date',''))[:10]
    guests=[a.get('email','').lower() for a in (e.get('attendees') or []) if not a.get('self')]
    summary=e.get('summary') or ''
    hit=next((g for g in guests if g in EMAIL2SLUG), None)
    if hit:
        slug,name=EMAIL2SLUG[hit]
        for i,a in enumerate(att):
            suffix = f"-{i+1}" if len(att)>1 else ""
            plan.append({"slug":slug,"name":name,"date":date,"fileId":a.get('fileId'),
                         "summary":summary,"email":hit,
                         "path":f"students/{slug}/transcript-downloads/{date}-{slug}-notes-by-gemini{suffix}.md"})
        continue
    c=next((g for g in guests if g in CONSULT), None)
    if c: consults.append({"date":date,"name":CONSULT[c],"email":c,"fileId":att[0].get('fileId'),"summary":summary})
    else: unknown.append({"date":date,"summary":summary,"guests":guests})
print(json.dumps({"plan":plan,"consults":consults,"unknown":unknown}))
PY

for k,v in c.most_common(): print(f"  {k:<20} {v}")
print("CONSULTATIONS:", len(d['consults']), [x['name'] for x in d['consults']])

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Extract compact digests from every transcript
command: python - <<'PY' > /tmp/digests.md
import glob,os,re
NEW=["celia-cutler","steven-morris","vinay-kalva","asia","fernande-charleus","whit-wilson","charles-moore"]
RECENT={"adriana-varea":["2026-08","2026-09"],"yahunatan-zetvy":["2026-08","2026-09"],"sofia":["2026-08","2026-09"]}
for slug in NEW+list(RECENT):
    files=sorted(glob.glob(f"students/{slug}/transcript-downloads/*.md"))
    if slug in RECENT:
        files=[f for f in files if any(p in os.path.basename(f) for p in RECENT[slug])]
    print(f"\n### {slug}")
    for f in files:
        t=open(f,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read()
        lines=[l.strip() for l in t.split("\n")]
        # the one-line summary sits after the email line
        summ=""
        for i,l in enumerate(lines[:16]):
            if "@" in l and i+1 < len(lines):
                rest=[x for x in lines[i+1:i+6] if x]
                if rest: summ=rest[0]
                break
        # topic headings: non-bullet, non-empty lines before the transcript body
        cut=t.find("Transcript")
        head=t[:cut if cut>0 else 6000]
        heads=[l for l in head.split("\n") if l.strip() and not l.strip().startswith(("*","-","•")) ][:40]
        topics=[h.strip() for h in heads if 8<len(h.strip())<70 and not h.strip().endswith(".") and "@" not in h][2:9]
        print(f"- **{os.path.basename(f)[:10]}** {summ[:190]}")
        if topics: print(f"    topics: {' | '.join(topics[:6])[:230]}")
PY
wc -l /tmp/digests.md; head -40 /tmp/digests.md

import glob,os,re
NEW=["celia-cutler","steven-morris","vinay-kalva","asia","fernande-charleus","whit-wilson","charles-moore"]
RECENT={"adriana-varea":["2026-08","2026-09"],"yahunatan-zetvy":["2026-08","2026-09"],"sofia":["2026-08","2026-09"]}
def digest(path):
    t=open(path,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read()
    cut=t.lower().find("\ntranscript")
    head=t[:cut] if cut>0 else t[:9000]
    lines=[l.rstrip() for l in head.split("\n")]
    # topic headings: a non-bullet line whose next non-empty line is a bullet
    topics=[]
    for i,l in enumerate(lines):
        s=l.strip()
        if not s or s.startswith(("*","•","-")) or "@" in s or len(s)>80: continue
        nxt=next((x.strip() for x in lines[i+1:i+3] if x.strip()), "")
        if nxt.startswith(("*","•")) and s.lower() not in ("attachments","meeting records","quick notes"):
            topics.append(s)
    # summary: longest plain sentence before the first topic
    stop=lines.index(topics[0]) if topics else len(lines)
    cands=[l.strip() for l in lines[:stop] if l.strip().endswith(".") and len(l.strip())>60]
    summ=max(cands,key=len) if cands else ""
    return summ, topics
for slug in NEW+list(RECENT):
    files=sorted(glob.glob(f"students/{slug}/transcript-downloads/*.md"))
    if slug in RECENT: files=[f for f in files if any(p in os.path.basename(f) for p in RECENT[slug])]
    print(f"\n### {slug}  ({len(files)} sessions)")
    for f in files:
        s,tp=digest(f)
        print(f"- **{os.path.basename(f)[:10]}** {s[:200] if s else '(no summary line)'}")
        if tp: print(f"    - {' | '.join(tp[:7])[:250]}")

command: export PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8; P=$(cygpath -w "$(pwd)/peek"); python - "$P" <<'PY'
import os,sys,re,glob,collections
for f in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(sys.argv[1],"*.txt"))):
    t=open(f,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read()
    names=collections.Counter(re.findall(r'^([A-Z][a-zA-Z\'\.]{1,18}(?: [A-Z][a-zA-Z\'\.]{1,18})?):', t, re.M))
    date=re.search(r'(\w{3} \d{1,2}, 20\d\d)', t[:400])
    print(f"{os.path.basename(f)[:12]} | {date.group(1) if date else '?':<14} | speakers: {names.most_common(4)}")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

import json,sys,re,collections
d=json.load(open(sys.argv[1],encoding='utf-8'))
STUDENT={"celiacutler@gmail.com":"Celia Cutler","adrianavarea9@gmail.com":"Adriana Varea",
"ynproemail@gmail.com":"Yahunathan Zetvy","sofiaminassian@gmail.com":"Sofia Minassian",
"workemail.vinay@gmail.com":"Vinay Kalva","hsmcharlotte@gmail.com":"Steven Morris",
"atamaami@gmail.com":"Asia Tamaami","fernandecharleus@gmail.com":"Fernande Charleus",
"whitwilson929@gmail.com":"Whit Wilson","moorecamooreca1@gmail.com":"Charles Moore",
"oreoluwavjames@gmail.com":"Oreoluwa","ivoryj.johnson3@gmail.com":"Ivory Johnson",
"amhuynh@asu.edu":"Amanda Huynh","avneet.2725@gmail.com":"Avneet Natt","davidsalgado":"David Salgado"}
sess=collections.defaultdict(list)
for e in d.get('items',[]):
    s=(e.get('summary') or '')
    if re.search(r'consultation', s, re.I): continue
    if not re.search(r'session|lsat|tutoring|/ joshua|joshua', s, re.I): continue
    st=(e.get('start',{}).get('dateTime') or e.get('start',{}).get('date',''))[:10]
    for a in (e.get('attendees') or []):
        em=(a.get('email') or '').lower()
        if em in STUDENT:
            sess[STUDENT[em]].append((st, bool(e.get('attachments'))))
print(f"{'Student':<20} {'sessions':>8} {'with notes':>11}")
tot=twn=0
for name,rows in sorted(sess.items(), key=lambda kv:-len(kv[1])):
    rows=sorted(set(rows))
    wn=sum(1 for _,a in rows if a)
    tot+=len(rows); twn+=wn
    print(f"{name:<20} {len(rows):>8} {wn:>11}   {', '.join(d[5:] for d,_ in rows)}")
print(f"{'TOTAL':<20} {tot:>8} {twn:>11}")
PY
